2026-11-14 · All platforms · Open-source software

An expanded open-ended search across creative AI, with detailed guides for crochet/papercraft, image editing, neural audio, mobile media, video agents and editable 3D, plus additional eligible screened discoveries retained in the searchable pending-profile appendix. Earlier screened entries receive complete guides where the evidence supports them; first guides are not claims of launches today. This is source/documentation review: no discovered application was installed or run. Software, model weights, third-party assets, paid services and proprietary hosts are distinguished. Every platform is researched equally; each entry reports its actual documented route and leaves unpublished requirements unknown. Quality labels follow evidence rather than stars or guide length. Current licensing, setup and availability follow-ups update eight existing library assessments while preserving historical editions. Verified repository aliases are excluded from new-tool counts. Primary-source web research supplements all 104 observed fields; inaccessible sources, limited paper reads and partial ecosystem coverage remain explicit. Verified collection: 913/913 repository queries attempted; 913 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 75 bounded/truncated. All 25 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Separate from successful search queries, 48 watchlist fetches and 3 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Search and review counts

104 creative fields; 913/913 repository queries attempted; 25/25 model-task queries attempted. 61244 distinct source candidates and 1074 model leads. 40 detailed profiles and 61 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 75 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

Profiles are based on current primary documentation. Creative quality and performance claims are attributed to the developers unless hands-on testing is explicitly recorded. Unknown hardware requirements stay unknown. Code, model weights, hosted services, and required proprietary hosts can have different terms.

StitchProof

Docs checked 2026-11-14 · 0 GitHub stars · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Review stitch-count arithmetic in permitted US-notation amigurumi or flat-row drafts, inspect each parsed row and export an HTML/Markdown review.

Evidence checked 2026-11-14; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: The repository provides an app video, screenshots and synthetic evaluations. Its headline fine-tuned run caught 43/45 seeded errors but also flagged 127/571 clean rows. A held-out “more times” notation caused 115/118 false alarms. These are author experiments, not real-designer or independent validation. [1] [3] [4]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Use Python 3.11+ and the app extras. The README starts a llama.cpp server with a base GGUF plus a separate LoRA, then FastAPI. Model preparation uses Tinker and the export extra; the displayed app/dev install does not include that optional extra. The exporter reads train/runs.json and requests its checkpoint through the authenticated Tinker account, so access to the author run is not established for another user. Reproduce/train a permitted run or use the clearly labeled recorded-answer demo while resolving this gap. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1] [3] [4]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.

  • Models, services and costs · unknown: MIT project code. Qwen and Gemma weight terms, llama.cpp/Ollama and Tinker access remain separate. The best LoRA download requires checkpoint access not established by a public source clone. Runtime can be local after the model files are available. Training/export may require separately authorized Tinker access and charges; the author’s past experiment cost is not a current price quote. [1] [3] [4]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Checks stitch arithmetic, not gauge, fit, appearance or a finished object. Synthetic-only training/evaluation and unsupported UK terms, lace, multi-size, short-row and Tunisian patterns limit applicability. Recorded answers are not a live model demo; checkpoint access and complete reproduction remain unresolved.

How it uses AI

A fine-tuned Qwen3.5-4B translates crochet rows into structured stitch programs; ordinary Python simulates their counts and produces fixed explanations. Optional local Gemma 4 transcribes photos for correction before checking. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

A fine-tuned Qwen3.5-4B translates crochet rows into structured stitch programs; ordinary Python simulates their counts and produces fixed explanations. Optional local Gemma 4 transcribes photos for correction before checking. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Review stitch-count arithmetic in permitted US-notation amigurumi or flat-row drafts, inspect each parsed row and export an HTML/Markdown review. [1] [3] [4]

Demo & examples

The repository provides an app video, screenshots and synthetic evaluations. Its headline fine-tuned run caught 43/45 seeded errors but also flagged 127/571 clean rows. A held-out “more times” notation caused 115/118 false alarms. These are author experiments, not real-designer or independent validation. [1] [3] [4]

Install

Use Python 3.11+ and the app extras. The README starts a llama.cpp server with a base GGUF plus a separate LoRA, then FastAPI. Model preparation uses Tinker and the export extra; the displayed app/dev install does not include that optional extra. The exporter reads train/runs.json and requests its checkpoint through the authenticated Tinker account, so access to the author run is not established for another user. Reproduce/train a permitted run or use the clearly labeled recorded-answer demo while resolving this gap. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use Python 3.11+ and the app extras. The README starts a llama.cpp server with a base GGUF plus a separate LoRA, then FastAPI. Model preparation uses Tinker and the export extra; the displayed app/dev install does not include that optional extra. The exporter reads train/runs.json and requests its checkpoint through the authenticated Tinker account, so access to the author run is not established for another user. Reproduce/train a permitted run or use the clearly labeled recorded-answer demo while resolving this gap.
git clone https://github.com/HeetRanpura/stitchproof.git
cd stitchproof
uv venv --python 3.11
uv pip install -e ".[app,dev,tinker]"
STITCHPROOF_BACKEND=replay python -m stitchproof.app.server

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4]

  1. For the supplied demo, open localhost:7860 and select the sample; replay mode shows recorded model answers rather than live inference.
  2. For new patterns, first establish access to the required base/adapter and start the documented llama-server on localhost:8080. Review the exported files and selected model terms.
  3. Paste a small permitted draft, inspect the parsed programs and editable photo transcription, then review the numbers behind ERROR or CANNOT CHECK before exporting a report.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4]

Hardware
Author measurement for one Q4 base/LoRA run: 2.93 GB model disk and 3.18 GB RAM on M4 MacBook; these are not universal minima or total setup space. Optional photo model and conversion/training files add resources. Numeric VRAM limits are not documented.
Software
Python >=3.11; FastAPI/Uvicorn app extras; llama.cpp for live parsing. Tinker/tinker-cookbook are separate export/training extras. Node is needed only for frontend development. Ollama with gemma4:e4b-it-qat is optional for photos.
Platforms
The documented inference/export example uses Metal on an M4 MacBook. A validated Windows/Linux/GPU support matrix is not documented; a browser frontend does not establish phone-local inference.

  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT project code. Qwen and Gemma weight terms, llama.cpp/Ollama and Tinker access remain separate. The best LoRA download requires checkpoint access not established by a public source clone.
  • Cost: Runtime can be local after the model files are available. Training/export may require separately authorized Tinker access and charges; the author’s past experiment cost is not a current price quote.

How it uses AI

An optional DeepSeek fallback translates unfamiliar crochet clauses into a small stitch-symbol vocabulary. The same deterministic stitch-count verifier accepts or rejects that proposal before PNG and A4 chart rendering. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

An optional DeepSeek fallback translates unfamiliar crochet clauses into a small stitch-symbol vocabulary. The same deterministic stitch-count verifier accepts or rejects that proposal before PNG and A4 chart rendering. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Convert a permitted written crochet PDF into an inspectable symbol chart and print sheets, with unrecognized or inconsistent rows retained as UNVERIFIED. [1] [3] [4]

Demo & examples

A synthetic sample PDF and its rows.json fixture are supplied. Source tests describe page coverage and symbol positioning; they do not establish that arbitrary real patterns or physical garments are correct. [1] [3] [4]

Install

Create a Python 3.12+ environment, install requirements and Poppler tools, and start the local Flask app. Create an admin invitation with app/manage.py, redeem it locally and configure the documented authenticator. Add a private DeepSeek key only if the AI fallback is wanted; deterministic parsing works without it. [1] [3] [4]

  3. Inspect accepted symbols and every UNVERIFIED row; compare the PNG and A4 print sheets with the source before using them in a craft project.

How it uses AI

A coding-agent workflow unfolds simple mesh parts into UV dielines, asks an image model to paint those sheets, then folds them in Blender into textured low-poly GLBs with optional fold, face-expression and flow clips. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Introduction

A coding-agent workflow unfolds simple mesh parts into UV dielines, asks an image model to paint those sheets, then folds them in Blender into textured low-poly GLBs with optional fold, face-expression and flow clips. [1] [3] [4] [5]

What it is good for

Create editable papercraft-style props or small game scenes from a shape spec and review an atlas, folding animation and multiple renders. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Demo & examples

The author supplies crate/tree/character examples and short scene videos. Supplied painted textures allow a no-new-generation folding example. Author scene frame rates and render timings describe particular machines/scenes, not independent quality or hardware minima. [1] [3] [4] [5]

python3 scripts/glb_check.py examples/crate/export/*.glb

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Review the supplied spec, layout and painting; make a copy for your own small prop rather than changing the geometry after it is painted.
  2. For a new atlas, review the image request and model terms, generate it separately, then inspect cleanup/gate files and the four review views.
  3. Open the GLB in the destination engine and check its Fold clip, materials, alpha and any animation extensions. Physical foldability or printing readiness is not established by a successful GLB export.

Software
Python 3.10+ with NumPy, opencv-python-headless and Pillow; headless Blender 4.x. Image API access is used only for the painting step. A coding-agent host is optional for orchestrating the scripts.
Platforms
The README gives a macOS Blender executable example and Apple M1 Max scene measurements. It does not document a validated universal desktop OS/version matrix or numeric RAM/VRAM/total disk minima.

How it uses AI

A desktop/CLI wrapper runs RobustVideoMatting with recurrent frame states, then composes video backgrounds or exports an alpha-bearing ProRes 4444 file. The interface exposes inference downsampling and matte cleanup. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Introduction

A desktop/CLI wrapper runs RobustVideoMatting with recurrent frame states, then composes video backgrounds or exports an alpha-bearing ProRes 4444 file. The interface exposes inference downsampling and matte cleanup. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Prepare an editable video cut-out for compositing and compare edge quality across short clips before queuing longer footage. [1] [3] [4] [5]

First project

  3. Check alpha interpretation and playback in the intended compositor before processing a batch. A selectable 4K export is not a guarantee of flicker-free mattes or adequate VRAM on every GPU.

Sampling and RANSAC are stochastic. Heavy matching can motivate Tiny RoMa, but variant results and hardware requirements must not be transferred automatically.

How it uses AI

RoMa estimates dense image-to-image correspondences and certainty maps using learned features and geometric matching, with sampling and conversion to pixel-coordinate keypoints for downstream estimation. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

RoMa estimates dense image-to-image correspondences and certainty maps using learned features and geometric matching, with sampling and conversion to pixel-coordinate keypoints for downstream estimation. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Develop image-alignment, capture and photogrammetry experiments, inspect warped image pairs and reject bad correspondences before a separate reconstruction stage. [1] [3] [4]

Demo & examples

The repository supplies warp/matching demos and links its CVPR 2024 evaluation. The Tiny RoMa comparison is a separate variant; its table is not a score for full RoMa or proof that a particular reconstruction will be accurate. [1] [3] [4]

Install

Use an isolated Python environment; the README reports testing on Linux/Python 3.12 and offers uv sync or installation of romatch. The source declares Python >=3.9 and Torch >=2.5.1. Begin with the bundled Toronto demo, resolving the chosen model downloads and optional acceleration separately. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use an isolated Python environment; the README reports testing on Linux/Python 3.12 and offers uv sync or installation of romatch. The source declares Python >=3.9 and Torch >=2.5.1. Begin with the bundled Toronto demo, resolving the chosen model downloads and optional acceleration separately.
git clone https://github.com/Parskatt/RoMa.git
cd RoMa
uv sync
uv run python demo/demo_match.py --im_A_path assets/toronto_A.jpg --im_B_path assets/toronto_B.jpg --save_path demo/roma_warp_toronto.jpg

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Run the documented sample only after preparing the model/environment, then compare the two source images with the certainty-weighted warp.
  2. Use the API to sample correspondences and convert normalized coordinates to pixels; inspect confidence and geometric inliers before estimating a transform.
  3. For owned capture imagery, vary resolution/thresholds and compare failures. Dense matches alone do not establish measured scale, a complete mesh or fabrication-ready geometry.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python >=3.9, Torch >=2.5.1 and the pyproject dependencies; Linux/Python 3.12 is the stated tested route. The optional fused-local-correlation extra is declared for Linux. Demo code selects CUDA, CPU or available MPS, without establishing equal support/performance.
Platforms
Documented testing is Linux/Python 3.12. The demo contains an MPS branch and CPU/CUDA selection, but independent Mac/Windows validation is not established. Minimum RAM, VRAM, storage and exact GPU capability are not documented.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4]

  • Weights: MIT covers the authors’ code except the separately Apache-2.0 DINOv2 component. Selected checkpoint, dataset and upstream feature-model terms must be reviewed separately; the complete runtime stack is not one MIT grant.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT covers the authors’ code except the separately Apache-2.0 DINOv2 component. Selected checkpoint, dataset and upstream feature-model terms must be reviewed separately; the complete runtime stack is not one MIT grant.
  • Cost: Local model inference/storage; no mandatory paid generation service is documented. Training and dataset preparation are separate workloads.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3] [4]

Limitations

Current code differs from the paper-era setup in scaling, Torch versions and a matrix decomposition. Sampling and RANSAC are stochastic. Heavy matching can motivate Tiny RoMa, but variant results and hardware requirements must not be transferred automatically. [1] [3] [4]

First project

  2. Ask the connected model for a small slide or visual panel using permitted assets; allow or deny snapshots on the TV and inspect the result.
  3. Review model/layout errors and stop the assistant’s content with Back on the remote when needed. Unlink/revoke the intended assistant before reusing a shared display.

How it uses AI

An unofficial MIT adaptation runs a reproduced TCSinger 2 singing model through neural.gen~ and ExecuTorch/MLX inside Max. Notes, lyrics, a style caption and an editable pitch curve become synthesized singing. [1] [3] [6] [5]

Introduction

An unofficial MIT adaptation runs a reproduced TCSinger 2 singing model through neural.gen~ and ExecuTorch/MLX inside Max. Notes, lyrics, a style caption and an editable pitch curve become synthesized singing. [1] [3] [6] [5]

What it is good for

Compose short synthetic vocal phrases and edit note timing or pitch contours in a Max patch or Max for Live device, with separately permitted voice and score inputs. [1] [3] [6] [5]

Demo & examples

The repository links a Max interface and the original TCSinger 2 demo. Its exported checkpoint is a reproduction trained on four named singing datasets, not the official original checkpoint. Published research comparisons do not independently validate this export or its real-time reliability. [1] [3] [6] [5]

Install

For inference, use Apple Silicon/macOS 14+ and Max 9+ with neural.gen~ installed. Download the exported model/tokenizer files from the linked model repository into export/max, or use the device download button. Open the bundled TCSinger2_UI.amxd or Max patches. Training and re-export are separate Linux/CUDA or Mac/Python workflows; they are not needed merely to load the provided export. [1] [3] [6] [5]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. For inference, use Apple Silicon/macOS 14+ and Max 9+ with neural.gen~ installed. Download the exported model/tokenizer files from the linked model repository into export/max, or use the device download button. Open the bundled TCSinger2_UI.amxd or Max patches. Training and re-export are separate Linux/CUDA or Mac/Python workflows; they are not needed merely to load the provided export.
git clone https://github.com/jasper-zheng/tcsinger2-maxmsp.git

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [6] [5]

  1. Open the device/patch with the exported files available to neural.gen~ and confirm both score2f0 and sing load.
  2. Enter notes and lyrics in the piano roll or read a JSON score; set tempo explicitly before importing a score because the piano roll retains its current tempo.
  3. Generate a short phrase, listen for pronunciation and timing errors, then edit the F0 curve and compare. More export sampling steps increase latency; do not assume a live performance deadline is guaranteed.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [6] [5]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
macOS 14+, Apple Silicon, Max 9+ and neural.gen~ for the selected inference route. The exported pair is approximately 150 MB plus 2.4 GB, excluding tokenizer, runtime and loaded memory. Custom export requires a matching ExecuTorch 1.5.1 environment. Training describes Linux/Python 3.11/CUDA 12.1.
Platforms
The selected Max inference route is Apple Silicon/macOS. The separate training example used an NVIDIA RTX A6000 48 GB; that is a training observation, not the minimum for Mac inference. Windows/Linux Max inference is not documented. Minimum system RAM and total storage are not published.

First project

Kotlin Multiplatform/Gradle with Maven Central. Documented platform baselines: iOS 16.6+, Android API 26+, desktop JVM 21+, or a WebAssembly-capable browser. Native backends and features differ; video additionally requires a compatible loaded model/runtime.
Platforms
The project offers Android, iOS, desktop and WASM interfaces, but that does not establish identical acceleration or support for every media operation. Numeric RAM, VRAM, model storage and full feature-by-platform validation are not documented in the reviewed guide.

Llamatik logos, icons, onboarding artwork and store screenshots are explicitly proprietary under LICENSE-assets and are excluded from this guide’s reuse scope.   • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. The full MIT grant covers project source code, including the selected core API. Llamatik logos, icons, onboarding artwork and store screenshots are explicitly proprietary under LICENSE-assets and are excluded from this guide’s reuse scope. node tools/comfy-gen.mjs --prompt "an original illustrated landscape" --out ./out --n 4
python tools/comic-layout.py --src ./out --out ./page --mode 4koma

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Review the selected ComfyUI checkpoint and generation settings; write prompts and generate a small panel set.
  2. Place dialogue.json beside the images, using relative panel coordinates for balloons or sound-effect labels.
  3. Render 4koma, 3rows or one-panel pages, then inspect balloon collisions, text legibility, model artifacts and pagination before exporting or printing.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python with Pillow/requests/BeautifulSoup/curl_cffi as documented, Node 18+, a reachable ComfyUI server and a compatible diffusion checkpoint. Optional reference-conditioning nodes/models add their own dependencies.
Platforms
The scripts are clients of the separately configured ComfyUI runtime. A tested OS matrix, minimum Python version, RAM/VRAM and total model storage are not documented; these cannot be inferred from the client interface.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: The complete MIT software grant was reviewed. ComfyUI, diffusion checkpoints, optional LoRAs/IP-Adapter components, fonts and source imagery retain their own terms.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. The complete MIT software grant was reviewed. ComfyUI, diffusion checkpoints, optional LoRAs/IP-Adapter components, fonts and source imagery retain their own terms.
  • Cost: Local ComfyUI compute/storage or a separately arranged server; checkpoint and asset rights are separate.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3]

Limitations

The selected workflow is generation and page layout. It does not establish that a model preserves character identity or that 300-DPI metadata makes the page press-ready. Server/checkpoint defaults and required custom nodes must be checked for the intended model. [1] [3]

How it uses AI

A webcam application uses MediaPipe neural hand landmarks, geometric gesture rules and OpenCV rendering to switch and animate screen-based sci-fi visual objects. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

A webcam application uses MediaPipe neural hand landmarks, geometric gesture rules and OpenCV rendering to switch and animate screen-based sci-fi visual objects. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Prototype a gesture-controlled screen artwork or workshop interaction using transparent PNG sprites and visible hand-tracking feedback. [1] [3] [4]

Demo & examples

The README describes palm, fist, pinch and one-finger gestures selecting animated visuals. These are composited screen effects, not volumetric holograms, and no independent recognition-rate or latency result was established. [1] [3] [4]

Install

Use Python 3.10–3.13, preferably the documented 3.12, a webcam and an isolated environment. Install requirements.txt and start main.py. Windows and POSIX launchers also prepare the environment and fetch the MediaPipe hand model; review those separately before use. [1] [3] [4]

git clone https://github.com/Ebinezer-cloud/hand-gesture.git
cd hand-gesture
python -m venv .venv

python main.py

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Activate the created environment using the command appropriate to the OS before the pip/start commands, allow camera access and try one hand under steady light.
  2. Compare each gesture with its displayed classification; use D for debugging, H for the HUD and S to save a screenshot.
  3. Replace the documented sprite PNGs with permitted artwork and inspect the interaction on the actual camera/display. Performance controls and smoothing do not guarantee recognition across users.

Software
Python 3.10–3.13, MediaPipe >=0.10.30,<1.0, OpenCV >=4.8, NumPy >=1.24 and Pillow >=10. Optional pyttsx3 enables spoken feedback. Initial package/model download needs connectivity.
Platforms
The README states Windows 10/11, macOS and Linux. This review did not execute those routes. A webcam is required; minimum CPU, RAM, VRAM and total storage are not documented. The hand-model artifact is about 8 MB, not the complete installation size.

Several architectures/losses and pretrained-model expansion remain unchecked roadmap items; those do not alone make all existing modules experimental. Data alignment, configurations and perceptual evaluation need user work.

How it uses AI

An MIT PyTorch toolkit trains neural models to emulate audio effects from aligned dry/wet recordings, with snapshot and parameter-conditioned RNN/TCN families and comparison metrics. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

Introduction

An MIT PyTorch toolkit trains neural models to emulate audio effects from aligned dry/wet recordings, with snapshot and parameter-conditioned RNN/TCN families and comparison metrics. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

What it is good for

Research a learned distortion, amplifier or compressor approximation and inspect its waveform, spectral and transient errors on held-out audio. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

Demo & examples

The authors link a toolkit paper and neural-effect studies, and document comparison plots. A training loss or supported architecture does not establish perceptual transparency, real-time plugin performance or reliable extrapolation. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

Install

Install pyneuralfx in an isolated compatible Python environment. For the training framework, clone the source, enter frame_work, prepare aligned dry/wet WAVs and edit the supplied dataset/configuration paths. Select the configuration in main_snapshot.py or main_full.py before starting the corresponding training command. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install pyneuralfx in an isolated compatible Python environment. For the training framework, clone the source, enter frame_work, prepare aligned dry/wet WAVs and edit the supplied dataset/configuration paths. Select the configuration in main_snapshot.py or main_full.py before starting the corresponding training command.
python -m pip install pyneuralfx
git clone https://github.com/ytsrt66589/pyneuralfx.git
cd pyneuralfx/frame_work
python3 main_snapshot.py

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

  1. Collect or obtain permitted, aligned input/output recordings; use one fixed setting for a snapshot model or a parameter-labeled dataset for a full model.
  2. Follow the dataset document to split train/validation/test audio and set paths, sample rate, channels and the chosen architecture configuration.
  3. Train a small experiment, then compare held-out waveforms, spectra, transients and listening examples. The evalvis document currently has headings without a complete command guide, so additional experiment-specific evaluation setup is needed.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
The inspected pyproject declares Python ^3.9, Torch >=2.1.1,<3, NumPy ^1.26.2 and audio/plotting dependencies. Training-framework dev utilities may need additional dependencies. The declared CUDA 11.8 wheel source must be reconciled with the actual target platform.
Platforms
The package declares Torch dependencies for Linux, macOS and Windows; a demonstrated per-platform training matrix is not established. Minimum GPU/VRAM, RAM, training time and dataset/storage size are not documented.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

  • Weights: MIT covers toolkit code. Training recordings, third-party effect implementations, downloadable datasets and any external checkpoints retain separate terms. Modeling an effect does not grant rights to distribute its recordings or branding.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT covers toolkit code. Training recordings, third-party effect implementations, downloadable datasets and any external checkpoints retain separate terms. Modeling an effect does not grant rights to distribute its recordings or branding.
  • Cost: Local training/inference plus any separately licensed recordings or hardware used to acquire a dry/wet dataset.

How it uses AI

A GPL Obsidian plugin imports Samsung Notes handwriting as SVG and typed text as Markdown, with optional vision-model transcription and drawing descriptions for search and accessibility. [1]

Introduction

A GPL Obsidian plugin imports Samsung Notes handwriting as SVG and typed text as Markdown, with optional vision-model transcription and drawing descriptions for search and accessibility. [1]

What it is good for

Preserve and search handwritten creative notebooks while retaining original stroke backups and clearly marking machine-generated text. [1]

Demo & examples

The author reports handwriting-error measurements on 21 pages of personal notes. Those small first-party examples do not establish general OCR quality; the source explicitly lists pen-rendering and older-note limitations. [1]

Install

Use Windows 10/11 with Samsung Notes installed/syncing and Obsidian desktop >=1.11.4 installed with installer >=1.9.12. Install/enable Memo Bridge through the documented community-plugin route or review its release files manually. Optional transcription is off until a provider is chosen; a local vision model or separately billed API is required. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use Windows 10/11 with Samsung Notes installed/syncing and Obsidian desktop >=1.11.4 installed with installer >=1.9.12. Install/enable Memo Bridge through the documented community-plugin route or review its release files manually. Optional transcription is off until a provider is chosen; a local vision model or separately billed API is required.

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Choose the Samsung data location and a target vault folder; start with a limited date range and inspect imported SVG/Markdown against the source.
  2. Use the backfill dry-run before importing older notes. Keep original .sdocx versions and notice thumbnail-only or missing-page cases.
  3. Enable a chosen vision provider explicitly, transcribe a small note and correct recognition/alt-text errors. Preserve the generated-text provenance and separate enrichment files when the note was already edited.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Windows 10/11, the syncing Samsung Notes Windows app, Obsidian desktop >=1.11.4 and installer >=1.9.12/Electron >=37.3. A separately configured vision model is needed only for AI transcription/description.
Platforms
Windows desktop only; not mobile. The suggested local Qwen vision artifact is about 6 GB and optional GLM-OCR about 3 GB, not minimum system memory or full disk usage. CPU/GPU, RAM/VRAM and complete storage minima are not documented.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: The full GPL-3.0-or-later software grant was reviewed; bundled sdocx is GPL and xmldom MIT. Samsung Notes and Obsidian are separately licensed hosts, and chosen models/APIs retain their own terms.
  • Commercial: Software use follows GPL-3.0. The full GPL-3.0-or-later software grant was reviewed; bundled sdocx is GPL and xmldom MIT. Samsung Notes and Obsidian are separately licensed hosts, and chosen models/APIs retain their own terms.
  • Cost: Plugin use follows GPL. Required host/account access, local model compute and optional API requests remain separate; no current complete price comparison was established.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

Import is one-way and depends on a reverse-engineered format. Calligraphy pen pressure, pencil texture, backgrounds and some old multi-page notes are imperfect or unavailable. A local import is conventional; optional model transcription may be local or remote and is not automatically accurate. [1]

How it uses AI

An MIT React app optionally asks a local Llama vision model for approximate character body-part boxes, converts them into editable 3D primitives and derives row-by-row crochet instructions. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

Introduction

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5] [6]

  1. Keep the app and Ollama in separate running terminals, open the local Vite address and supply a permitted drawing or photo.
  2. Choose Analyze with AI, then correct the approximate boxes, labels and proportions in the segment/part editors.
  3. Inspect the primitive 3D model and generated row instructions; test a small crocheted part with the actual yarn/gauge before relying on size or assembly. Remote-provider examples require code adaptation and are not the default installed route.

Software
Compatible Node/npm plus the current Vite/React/Express dependency set. Optional local inference requires Ollama and llama3.2-vision; its documented download is about 7 GB, separate from loaded memory and the app. Express defaults to 3001, Vite to 5173, Ollama to 11434.
Platforms
An explicit independently tested OS matrix is not documented. A browser frontend does not prove universal model/driver support. Minimum system RAM, VRAM and complete storage are not specified; the model download size is not a RAM minimum.

How it uses AI

An MIT prototype kitchen-design workspace combines local editable box geometry with a Go/Baseten model service that proposes cabinet-width changes and describes uploaded reference photos for human review. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Introduction

An MIT prototype kitchen-design workspace combines local editable box geometry with a Go/Baseten model service that proposes cabinet-width changes and describes uploaded reference photos for human review. [1] [3] [4] [5]

What it is good for

Explore a coarse kitchen layout and an explicit propose/review/apply interaction, keeping approximate or user-entered dimensions distinct from verified measurements. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Demo & examples

The source documents resize proposals, local project files and fake-provider tests. A recorded placeholder-key request failed; this is not evidence of a reproduced live-model installation. Photo analysis does not measure the room, and AI Render is a placeholder. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Install

The documented development route uses Bun 1.3.8 and Go (the service declares 1.22). Clone, install and run dev to start Electron with its loopback Go child. Configure a separately authorized Baseten key privately for actual model calls. dev:web is only a browser preview without the service bridge or native project saving. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. The documented development route uses Bun 1.3.8 and Go (the service declares 1.22). Clone, install and run dev to start Electron with its loopback Go child. Configure a separately authorized Baseten key privately for actual model calls. dev:web is only a browser preview without the service bridge or native project saving.
git clone https://github.com/JaskirtKaler/RenovaCAD.git
cd RenovaCAD
bun install
bun run dev

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Start with local cabinet selection and a small manual edit, then inspect the scene’s units and dimension provenance.
  2. With authorized model access, request a cabinet-width change, review the before/after proposal and explicitly apply or cancel. A stale proposal must be requested again.
  3. Save a trial .renovacad file through Electron and reopen it. Treat photo-derived layout/dimensions as approximate, and do not use the preview as construction-ready CAD or a code-compliance check.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Bun 1.3.8, Go 1.22 as declared in go.mod, Electron and the repository dependencies. The selected AI service requires Baseten model entitlement; a separate Muse development launcher/key is not the application’s inference key.
Platforms
The project describes a macOS workspace. Distributable packaging/signing is unfinished; tested Windows/Linux desktop support and numeric RAM, GPU/VRAM or total storage minima are not established. Python is not required for the current UI.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4] [5]

  • Weights: MIT covers RenovaCAD code. Baseten/model access, Electron dependencies and supplied room/reference imagery have separate terms. An open-source client does not grant access to a restricted hackathon or provider model.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT covers RenovaCAD code. Baseten/model access, Electron dependencies and supplied room/reference imagery have separate terms. An open-source client does not grant access to a restricted hackathon or provider model.
  • Cost: Local editing plus separately authorized Baseten usage. Current model access and pricing are unresolved; the placeholder is not a working credential.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Limitations

Current documentation calls this a first milestone and leaves packaging and photorealistic rendering unfinished. It also retains conflicting old “disconnected UI” wording; inspected preload/API documentation contains a real service bridge, but no successful live-model execution was established. Collision checks are limited and not a full CAD engine. [1] [3] [4] [5]

How it uses AI

An MIT JavaScript map-animation framework is explicitly designed for AI agents to author short scene files, obtain real geospatial data and render deterministic 2D/2.5D map videos. [1]

Introduction

An MIT JavaScript map-animation framework is explicitly designed for AI agents to author short scene files, obtain real geospatial data and render deterministic 2D/2.5D map videos. [1]

What it is good for

Prototype route explainers, place-based visual stories and animated data maps with editable scenes and visible source attribution. [1]

Demo & examples

The maintainer supplies ten example scenes/videos and a separate-agent authoring account. These are first-party demonstrations, not independent geographic-accuracy, performance or creative-quality verification. [1]

Install

Prepare Node 20+, FFmpeg on PATH and Chrome/Edge or a compatible Chromium installation. Clone and install dependencies, run the documented environment doctor, then scaffold a scene and inspect its contact sheet before rendering. A separately configured coding agent supplies the AI authoring; the renderer itself is conventional. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare Node 20+, FFmpeg on PATH and Chrome/Edge or a compatible Chromium installation. Clone and install dependencies, run the documented environment doctor, then scaffold a scene and inspect its contact sheet before rendering. A separately configured coding agent supplies the AI authoring; the renderer itself is conventional.
git clone https://github.com/MusicMaster4/map-bamba.git
cd map-bamba
npm install
node bin/bamba.js doctor
node bin/bamba.js new my-scene
node bin/bamba.js sheet scenes/my-scene.js
node bin/bamba.js render scenes/my-scene.js

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Give the model a permitted place/story brief and inspect the resulting scene’s locations, dates, labels and motion choices.
  2. Check the geocoded stops, route, map-data coverage and attribution; preview representative frames and correct unsupported factual claims.
  3. Render a short trial and retain its source/cache for revision. Community tile, routing and lookup services require considerate use; cached re-rendering is different from an offline first acquisition.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Node 20+, FFmpeg and Chrome/Edge/Chromium. MapLibre/WebGL and the renderer’s chosen encoder need working graphics/browser drivers. External-agent access and initial map/data requests are separate.
Platforms
Author testing used Windows 11 with an NVIDIA GPU; Linux/macOS share a less-tested code path. Numeric RAM, VRAM and total storage minima are not documented. A reported RTX 3070 render time is an observation, not a minimum or speed guarantee.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: MIT covers framework code. OpenStreetMap/OpenFreeMap/OSRM/Nominatim/Overpass data have separate attribution/use conditions; bundled fonts are OFL and terrain/example datasets have their own terms. Keep the renderer’s OpenStreetMap credit.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT covers framework code. OpenStreetMap/OpenFreeMap/OSRM/Nominatim/Overpass data have separate attribution/use conditions; bundled fonts are OFL and terrain/example datasets have their own terms. Keep the renderer’s OpenStreetMap credit.
  • Cost: Local rendering plus separately arranged agent access. Community geospatial services are described as free for light use; heavy production may require self-hosted or paid infrastructure.

AI scene authoring and real data do not ensure factual map interpretation or up-to-date establishments/routes. The 2.5D buildings are data-derived extrusions, not a survey-quality city capture. API caching does not remove upstream service limits. [1]

How it uses AI

An MIT local Rust/web application uses Whisper transcription, signal-based moment detection and optional language-model ranking to make captioned vertical clips from gameplay recordings. [1] [3]

Introduction

An MIT local Rust/web application uses Whisper transcription, signal-based moment detection and optional language-model ranking to make captioned vertical clips from gameplay recordings. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Review candidate highlights from permitted gameplay, adjust framing and export short-form edits without a mandatory cloud renderer. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The source describes analysis stages and encoding paths, not independent highlight-selection or engagement results. Optional model ranking can fall back to signal ranking on failure; a completed job does not prove its clips are the best moments. [1] [3]

Install

Prepare Rust, Bun, CMake and platform build tools, FFmpeg with libass and yt-dlp. Build the web frontend and run the Rust backend in release mode. The first transcription job downloads the Whisper model. Use Settings to check the detected encoder/dependencies before processing a long recording. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare Rust, Bun, CMake and platform build tools, FFmpeg with libass and yt-dlp. Build the web frontend and run the Rust backend in release mode. The first transcription job downloads the Whisper model. Use Settings to check the detected encoder/dependencies before processing a long recording.
git clone https://github.com/shardreel/clipforge.git
cd clipforge
cd web
bun install
bun run build
cd ..
cargo run --release

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Open 127.0.0.1:8787, check system/storage readiness and start with a short owned local video.
  2. Select blur/crop/fit or the beta facecam layout. Optional AI ranking needs a configured model/provider; local LM Studio/Ollama and hosted routes have different privacy/cost behavior.
  3. Watch the exported clips through their reaction endings, inspect word captions and framing, and revise layout. Cached analysis avoids repeating transcription for a re-render.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
Documented startup minima: 8 GB RAM and 10 GB free working space; recommended 16 GB RAM/30 GB disk. Linux can use CPU, while Mac requires Apple Silicon. The source does not publish a universal minimum VRAM.
Software
Rust/Bun/CMake and the documented platform build dependencies; FFmpeg/libass and yt-dlp. Whisper large-v3-turbo is about 1.6 GB. macOS needs Apple Silicon; Linux can transcribe on CPU, with CUDA optional.
Platforms
Apple Silicon macOS and Linux are documented; Windows uses WSL2/Linux rather than a native build. Intel Macs are unsupported. Startup requires 8 GB RAM and 10 GB free working space, recommending 16 GB/30 GB. Four CPU cores are a warning threshold, not a hard job minimum.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: MIT covers the application. Whisper artifacts, FFmpeg builds, model services and source gameplay/streamer material have separate rights. A clip export does not grant permission to repost another creator’s footage.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT covers the application. Whisper artifacts, FFmpeg builds, model services and source gameplay/streamer material have separate rights. A clip export does not grant permission to repost another creator’s footage.
  • Cost: Local transcription/rendering with optional separately billed hosted ranking; no required application subscription is documented.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3]

Limitations

Facecam framing is beta and optional ranking is fallible. A local renderer does not make hosted ranking private/offline. Minimum VRAM and a guaranteed processing rate are not documented; large recordings require more storage than the startup threshold. [1] [3]

How it uses AI

An MIT Windows-first desktop client uses a local TripoSR environment to infer a 3D product from one photo and render a short portrait turntable video. [1] [3]

Introduction

An MIT Windows-first desktop client uses a local TripoSR environment to infer a 3D product from one photo and render a short portrait turntable video. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Explore a quick product-spin concept while treating unseen surfaces as model guesses rather than an accurate product capture. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The README distinguishes single-photo AI modeling from ordinary multi-photo transitions. The latter does not reconstruct hidden sides, and no independent geometric or product-fidelity result was established. [1] [3]

Install

For source development prepare Node 20+, Rust/MSVC, Microsoft C++ Build Tools, Python 3.11, Git and WebView2. Install the desktop dependencies and start the Tauri app. In a separate PowerShell window run the documented ai_service installer; it uses CPU PyTorch by default, with a matching official wheel index needed for NVIDIA acceleration. git clone https://github.com/seanking19960301/360spin.git
cd 360spin
npm install
npm run desktop

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Prepare the separately documented local AI service before choosing Local AI modeling. First generation downloads the TripoSR weights, approximately 1.7 GB.
  2. Place the intended reference photo first; current modeling uses that image rather than fusing multiple views.
  3. Inspect the 288×512 portrait MP4 for shape, text and invented hidden details. Use Photo-view transitions only when that conventional mode is desired, without presenting it as a reconstructed 3D model.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Node 20+/npm, Rust stable/MSVC and C++ Build Tools, Python 3.11/Git, and Windows WebView2. The AI service is a separate environment rather than a complete one-click installer dependency.
Platforms
Windows-first with Windows 10/11/WebView2 described. An NVIDIA GPU with about 6 GB VRAM is recommended, not required; CPU inference is documented as slower. Tested Mac/Linux support, minimum system RAM and total storage are not documented.

  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT covers the application. TripoSR/model artifacts and third-party packages retain separate grants; the reviewed root license is not a complete runtime/model rights audit.

Unmesh

Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prototype a parametric reconstruction or editable mechanical prop from an owned scan, with deviations and failed fits explicitly inspected.

Evidence checked 2026-11-14; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Why Experimental: The current README explicitly marks this scan-to-CAD application alpha and warns of rough edges, with the Mac GUI and some interaction behavior not validated. [1] [3]
  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: The author publishes several acceptance jobs and scan-deviation percentages. These are first-party tests; valid-solid checks and STEP read-back do not independently prove manufacturing fit or every free-form reconstruction. [1] [3]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Use Python 3.11, a 64-bit OS and an OpenGL 3.2+ driver. Install the editable app and its ai extra in an isolated environment, then open a scan through python -m unmesh. For model control enable the documented Let Claude drive route or run its MCP server in a compatible client. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1] [3]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
  • Models, services and costs · unknown: The complete AGPL-3.0 software grant was reviewed; the README specifies AGPL-3.0-only. Adapted ScanRuler work and CAD/GUI dependencies retain their notices. External-agent/model and scan rights remain separate. Local geometry processing plus separately configured model-agent access. No mandatory Unmesh service account is documented. [1] [3]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The current source explicitly labels the application alpha with rough edges. Scan agreement is not a calibration or fabrication certificate. Trackpad gestures and the Mac GUI remain unresolved, and free-form or difficult parts can remain not done.

How it uses AI

An AGPL scan-to-CAD application exposes its geometry operations through MCP so a model agent can reconstruct editable solids from a scan and compare them against a chosen tolerance. [1] [3]

Introduction

An AGPL scan-to-CAD application exposes its geometry operations through MCP so a model agent can reconstruct editable solids from a scan and compare them against a chosen tolerance. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Prototype a parametric reconstruction or editable mechanical prop from an owned scan, with deviations and failed fits explicitly inspected. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The author publishes several acceptance jobs and scan-deviation percentages. These are first-party tests; valid-solid checks and STEP read-back do not independently prove manufacturing fit or every free-form reconstruction. [1] [3]

Install

Use Python 3.11, a 64-bit OS and an OpenGL 3.2+ driver. Install the editable app and its ai extra in an isolated environment, then open a scan through python -m unmesh. For model control enable the documented Let Claude drive route or run its MCP server in a compatible client. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use Python 3.11, a 64-bit OS and an OpenGL 3.2+ driver. Install the editable app and its ai extra in an isolated environment, then open a scan through python -m unmesh. For model control enable the documented Let Claude drive route or run its MCP server in a compatible client.
git clone https://github.com/bit-spirit/unmesh.git
cd unmesh
python -m venv .venv
python -m pip install -e ".[ai]"
python -m unmesh

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Activate the new environment before installing, open an owned scan, align it and choose a reconstruction tolerance appropriate to the measurement evidence.
  2. Let the configured model plan and invoke geometry tools, or edit manually, then inspect whole-scan deviation and the history after each group.
  3. Export a trial STEP and inspect read-back plus actual critical dimensions. A local “AI reconstruction” button is not by itself evidence of neural inference; the explicit external-agent/MCP path is this guide’s AI scope.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
64-bit system with OpenGL 3.2+; author estimate roughly 2 GB RAM per million scan triangles. Minimum total RAM, dedicated VRAM and disk are not documented.
Software
Python 3.11, the source-declared Qt/VTK/OpenCASCADE/Open3D/SolveSpace stack, 64-bit OS and OpenGL 3.2+. A model/MCP client is separate for AI-driven authoring. The README estimates about 2 GB RAM per million scan triangles.
Platforms
Developed/used on Windows; Linux tests run in CI. Apple Silicon macOS CI excludes the main window, which the author says has not been used on a real Mac. Total RAM/VRAM/storage minima are not documented; the triangle-memory estimate scales with input.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: The complete AGPL-3.0 software grant was reviewed; the README specifies AGPL-3.0-only. Adapted ScanRuler work and CAD/GUI dependencies retain their notices. External-agent/model and scan rights remain separate.
  • Commercial: Software use follows AGPL-3.0. The complete AGPL-3.0 software grant was reviewed; the README specifies AGPL-3.0-only. Adapted ScanRuler work and CAD/GUI dependencies retain their notices. External-agent/model and scan rights remain separate.
  • Cost: Local geometry processing plus separately configured model-agent access. No mandatory Unmesh service account is documented.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3]

Limitations

The current source explicitly labels the application alpha with rough edges. Scan agreement is not a calibration or fabrication certificate. Trackpad gestures and the Mac GUI remain unresolved, and free-form or difficult parts can remain not done. [1] [3]

How it uses AI

A dual-licensed live visualizer exposes MediaPipe/YOLO vision bridges and a MiDaS depth route so learned hand, pose, face or object signals can control its shader and particle layers. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

A dual-licensed live visualizer exposes MediaPipe/YOLO vision bridges and a MiDaS depth route so learned hand, pose, face or object signals can control its shader and particle layers. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Prototype a camera-controlled live projection or VJ scene, mapping a hand landmark to distortion or using neural depth as one input to the image. Ordinary FFT/beat reactivity alone is not the AI claim here. [1] [3] [4]

Demo & examples

The author publishes effect clips, layered examples and bridge documentation. The demos and first-party compatibility statements have not established independent performance or a reliable end-to-end installation. [1] [3] [4]

Install

Obtain the official macOS, Windows or Linux release, or build with the documented Rust 1.97+ toolchain. For the neural hand-control route prepare an isolated Python environment, install bridges/requirements-vision.txt, start Fosfora and run the MediaPipe hand bridge. The source commands below are alternatives to a release install, not steps to run both copies. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Obtain the official macOS, Windows or Linux release, or build with the documented Rust 1.97+ toolchain. git clone https://github.com/kevinraymond/fosfora.git
cd fosfora
cargo run --release
python -m pip install -r bridges/requirements-vision.txt
python bridges/mediapipe_hands.py

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Activate your isolated Python environment before installing bridge dependencies. Start Fosfora, then start the hand bridge in a separate terminal with webcam access.
  2. Press B to open the Binding Matrix and connect a hand X-position source to one visible parameter such as warp intensity. Observe latency and false or missing tracks in the actual lighting.
  3. Choose an appropriate output: Spout on Windows, Syphon on macOS, or separately installed NDI. Default local bridge networking differs from another-computer/container access, which requires explicitly enabling Other devices and keeping its access key private.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Source build: Rust 1.97+ and a supported Vulkan/Metal GPU path. Vision bridges require Python and their separate requirements file; an exact supported Python range is not stated in the reviewed bridge guide. Optional depth source build needs the documented depth feature and system libraries.
Platforms
Official builds are offered for macOS, Windows and Linux. The bridge guide documents native and container routes with platform caveats; these were not reproduced here. Minimum RAM, VRAM, storage and guaranteed frame rate are not documented.

How it uses AI

An Apache-licensed Android comic reader offers model-based CPU/GPU/NPU enhancement at image decode, including AnimeVideo and Omni model options. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Introduction

An Apache-licensed Android comic reader offers model-based CPU/GPU/NPU enhancement at image decode, including AnimeVideo and Omni model options. [1] [3] [4] [5]

What it is good for

Compare the readability of owned comic pages on a phone or tablet, without treating enhanced display pixels as an authenticated restoration or a print-ready master. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Demo & examples

The source describes model-dependent line enhancement and warns that NPU models can shift colors. No independent before/after fidelity study was established. The release and build file are at 1.7.0 while both reviewed README headers still say 1.6.0. [1] [3] [4] [5]

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Open an owned local comic/archive, or configure your own Komga, WebDAV or SMB source. Komga supplies page conversion on that separate server route.
  2. Enable the chosen enhancement model and allow first-use GPU/NPU initialization to finish. The author recommends GPU tile size 192, which is a tuning value rather than a universal memory requirement.
  3. Compare the enhanced view with the source at an appropriate zoom, especially line shape, text and colors. AnimeVideo is suggested for color pages and Omni-Mini for monochrome; stronger/slower models and NPU color shifts remain device-dependent.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Android 8.0+ is documented. Release v1.7.0 includes arm64-v8a and universal APK assets. Local archive support and server-format conversion differ; model packs are separately downloaded.
Platforms
Android phones, tablets and large-screen Android devices are documented. Minimum RAM, accelerator capability, total storage and supported GPU/NPU model combinations are not comprehensively specified. There is no documented native macOS, Windows or iOS app route.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4] [5]

  • Weights: The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed. Model packs, adapted reader components and external server/media content retain their own notices and terms; the model dependencies have not received a complete rights audit here.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed. Model packs, adapted reader components and external server/media content retain their own notices and terms; the model dependencies have not received a complete rights audit here.
  • Cost: Local reader/enhancement compute plus any separately operated storage/server and model downloads; no mandatory application subscription is documented.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Limitations

This is decode-stage viewing enhancement, not a documented master-file restoration/export pipeline. NPU color shifts and model/device tradeoffs are explicit. The reader provides no comic content or permission to access another person’s collection. [1] [3] [4] [5]

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Create a small Windows application referencing the intended source module. Start with the documented image or upscale example and replace every sample model path with an authorized local artifact.

  3. Inspect the result and preserve timing/audio deliberately. The ONNX vision README says its frame-only media path needs a separate audio restore step; do not generalize that limitation to the StableDiffusionCpp VideoSequence example, which carries the input audio object.

Laplace learned contour drawing

How it uses AI

An MIT PyTorch script learns a damped-harmonic representation of contours extracted from a supplied line image, then exports the fitted path and a progressive drawing animation. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Introduction

An MIT PyTorch script learns a damped-harmonic representation of contours extracted from a supplied line image, then exports the fitted path and a progressive drawing animation. [1] [3] [4] [5]

What it is good for

Explore a compact learned line trajectory for a computational drawing study, comparing reconstruction error and visual continuity against an owned source image. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Demo & examples

The repository contains SVG, MP4 and analysis examples. The selected script fits one image using FFT initialization and gradient optimization; it does not establish a general image generator, superiority over other architectures or safe physical robot motion. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Install

Clone the repository, create and activate an isolated Python environment, install the pinned requirements and provide FFmpeg for H.264 animation export. SciPy 1.16.2 in that requirements file requires Python 3.11+ according to its current PyPI metadata; exact whole-project wheel/platform compatibility is not established. Run the actual checked-in filename below, rather than the different filename in the script header. [1] [3] [4] [5]

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Activate the new environment before installing. Start with a simple owned line image and inspect contour extraction; automatic stitching can connect separate contours in an unintended order.
  2. The script trains per input and chooses CUDA when torch.cuda.is_available(), otherwise CPU. Adjust points, units, epochs and edge thresholds deliberately; the default is 3000 points, 300 units and 6000 epochs, not a performance guarantee.
  3. Inspect laplace_drawing.svg, laplace_analysis.png and the animation in a separate output copy. The duration option controls the learned trajectory time, while the animation creates one frame per point at 60 fps; it is not a verified output-length setting. The code can fall back to GIF after FFmpeg failure.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Pinned scientific Python dependencies include PyTorch 2.9.0, NumPy 2.2.6 and SciPy 1.16.2. The SciPy package floor is Python 3.11+. OpenCV extracts contours, Matplotlib plots/animates, and FFmpeg is used for H.264 output.
Platforms
The selected code has CPU and CUDA branches, but an end-to-end tested operating-system matrix is not documented. Minimum RAM, VRAM, disk and runtime are unspecified. CUDA availability is not a statement that every package or platform combination works.

  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT covers the project source. This per-input fitting route does not require a pretrained model download. Scientific/encoding dependencies and rights in the supplied image remain separate.
  • Cost: Local compute and optional acceleration; no required hosted model service is documented for this selected script.
